next three columns remove one friction at a time. The last column removes all three frictions. See Table B2 for moments where
each friction is included on its own.
Below we explain the effect of each of the three frictions:10

### Page 17

14
• Convex recruiting costs: The convexity of recruiting costs moderates the response of hiring
to fluctuations in match value. Without this convexity, hiring responds much more to fluctuations
in match value, so the UE rate is too volatile (see the fifth column in Table 2 and the yellow lines
in Figure 5). After a negative shock, UE flows initially decline over the first year and the EU rate
is near steady state shortly after the initial spike. This reduces the skewness in unemployment

rate changes.
• Insecurely attached workers: The presence of insecurely attached workers is the most
significant contributor to the skewness in unemployment rate changes (see the fourth column in
Table 2 and the red lines in Figure 5). It does so in two ways. First, the layoff rate for securely
attached employees provides a highly nonlinear component to the EU rate. In steady state, very
few securely attached employees are laid off and the mass of employees near the layoff threshold

is small. Therefore, layoffs vary little for positive or small negative shocks. But when large negative
shocks occur, the mass of employees above the layoff threshold is much larger, so there is a
spike in layoffs. This helps generates asymmetric spikes in unemployment, although it does not
generate persistence in the EU rate once match values recover.11 But this is where the insecurely
attached component of layoffs helps. Their layoff rate is much higher, so when unemployment is

high and the flow of hires out of unemployment rises above steady state, the layoff rate also rises.
The unemployed may cycle through several short-term jobs before regaining secure employment.
This feature substantially slows the recovery in unemployment and helps generate persistence
and skewness in the EU rate.
• Asymmetric nominal wage stickiness: After a positive shock, nominal and real wages growth
increases, offsetting a lot of the increase in marginal revenue product. This moderates and

shortens the increase in match values for firms, dampening the incentive to increase employment
or any reduction in layoffs. In contrast, after a negative shock nominal wages growth does
not decrease very much. In the case of a negative demand shock, real wages growth actually
increases (due to the decline in inflation). This both amplifies and prolongs the decline in match
values for firms, which helps to generate both the asymmetric plucks and the slow recoveries in

unemployment (see the third column in Table 2 and the blue lines in Figure 5).
4. Phillips curve and NAIRU
4.1 Nonlinear Phillips curve
The model implies that the relationship between inflation and unemployment (i.e. the Phillips
curve) is nonlinear and state-dependent. Figures 6 and 7 show this relationship by plotting inflation
against unemployment conditional on different initial values for unemployment or real wages.12 The
Phillips curve is much steeper at low rates of unemployment, but this steepening occurs at higher

10 Dupraz et al (2025) also include insecurely attached workers and downward nominal wage rigidities to generate
asymmetry in unemployment dynamics.
11 The layoff rate for securely attached employees moves contemporaneously with match values, and is therefore only as
persistent as match values.
12 Specifically, we vary the policy/demand shock variable and plot the maximum deviation of inflation from target against
the unemployment rate for each value of this shock. This accounts for the dynamic relationship between inflation and

unemployment in the model, which is largely not contemporaneous. The peak unemployment response tends to be
quite lagged, because the model includes many labour-market frictions, but inflation responds very rapidly, because
expectations are forward-looking and based on full information. Introducing features like indexation, backward-looking
expectations, inattention or imperfect information could slow the inflation response.

### Page 18

15
Figure 5: Response to aggregate demand shocks with different versions of model
Note: There are two shocks for each model: the largest positive and largest negative shocks from Figure 4. The
economy is initially at steady state.
Figure 6: Phillips curve given initial unemployment
Note: The Phillips curves are constructed by varying the demand shock and plotting the inflation and
unemployment outcomes that it traces out. The dashed vertical line is steady-state unemployment.

unemployment rates when the initial unemployment rate is higher. When the initial level of real wages
is higher, the Phillips curve shifts upwards.
4.2 Short-run NAIRU
Figures 6 and 7 show that the unemployment rate consistent with maintaining inflation at target
(i.e. the intersection of the Phillips curves with the x-axis) is state-dependent. At steady state, it is

### Page 19

16
Figure 7: Phillips curve given initial level of real wages
Note: The Phillips curves are constructed by varying the demand shock and plotting the inflation and
unemployment outcomes that it traces out. The dashed vertical line is steady-state unemployment.
4.5 percent under our calibration of the model. But if unemployment or real wages increase, then
the Phillips curve shifts and the one-year-ahead unemployment forecast consistent with at-target

inflation increases.
We can therefore construct a ‘short-run NAIRU’ concept in our model. We define it as follows: if
policymakers wanted to eliminate any deviations in inflation from target stemming excessive or
insufficient demand in the labour market, what unemployment rate at a one-year horizon would
be required? This is essentially the intercept with the x-axis in Figures 6 and 7. At steady state, it
coincides with the long-run NIARU (i.e. the intersection of the steady-state Phillips curve in black

with the x-axis).
Figure 8 uses the model to calculate the historical path of the ‘short-run NAIRU’ in Australia from
1980 to 2025. It is calculated by: (i) calculating the sequence of demand shocks that in our model
replicates the historical path of the unemployment rate;13 (ii) using the model to simulate the inflation
generated by this sequence of demand shocks, which we define as the ‘labour-market’ component of
inflation;14 (iii) given the state of the economy in each period, calculating the sequence of demand

shocks consistent with forecast inflation being at target; and (iv) calculating the the one-year-ahead
forecast for the unemployment rate given that sequence of shocks and defining it as as the ‘one-
year-ahead NAIRU’ for that period.15
13 For simplicity, we are assuming that historical unemployment has been driven by only demand shocks. It is likely that
other shocks also drove some of the variation in the unemployment rate.
14 This simulated inflation series is in Figure B1.

15 We are ignoring here potential interactions between supply-driven inflation and the labour market. For example, an
inflationary supply shock might make it easier to reduce real wages, allowing the labour-market-driven component of
inflation to fall without as much of an increase in unemployment.

### Page 20

17
Figure 8: Short-run NAIRU
Note: The long-run NAIRU estimate is from Ballantyne and Cusbert (2025).
Figure 8 shows that the short-run NAIRU varies cyclically much more than the long-run NAIRU.16
When unemployment is above trend, the short-run NAIRU tends to increase and track the actual
unemployment rate. But there is some asymmetry: in the pre-GFC period when unemployment fell
below trend, the short-run NAIRU did not decline. In the recent period, the short-run NAIRU was

above the long-run NAIRU, even though actual unemployment was below it. In a tight labour market,
unemployment declines, which shifts the Phillips curve to the left, but real wages rise, which shifts
the Phillips curve up. The effect on the short-run NAIRU is ambiguous.
4.3 Long-run NAIRU
Although the dynamics of the model describe only cyclical fluctuations in unemployment, the model’s
long-run properties can be used to consider the determinants of the long-run NAIRU (i.e. the NAIRU

concept that is most commonly estimated by central banks using latent-variable models with a unit-
root NAIRU process, e.g. (Ballantyne and Cusbert 2025)).
The deterministic steady-state unemployment rate in the model is determined by parameters such
as recruiting costs, on-the-job search effort, and firms’ labour market monopsony power determine
the long-run NAIRU in the model. But this concept is not exactly analogous to the long-run NAIRU,
because it assumes that agents believe there will be no shocks in the future. The long-run NAIRU

is instead the stochastic steady-state unemployment rate, which is the unemployment rate in the
absence of shocks but assuming agents still believe shocks will occur in the future according to their
specified distribution.
Since we compute the global nonlinear solution to the model with rational expectations and aggregate
risk, we can consider how changes in cyclical properties of the model (e.g. shock volatility, price and
16 Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.) also discuss the concept of a short-run NAIRU that fluctuates cyclically.

### Page 21

18
Figure 9: Effect of parameters on the long-run NAIRU
Note: The dashed vertical line represents the calibration in the baseline model.
wage stickiness, monetary policy) affect the long-run NAIRU via agents’ response to expectations
and risk. Figure 9 shows how the long-run NAIRU changes when certain parameters are varied.
Increasing the weight on unemployment in the policy rule increases the long-run NAIRU. This may
reflect a kind of level-variance trade off: if policy stabilises labour demand to a greater extent, the

downward nominal wage rigidity will bind less often. Firms understand this decline in the risk of
incurring wage adjustment costs in the future, and are therefore more willing to set higher real
wages in steady state, which reduces match values and raises the level of unemployment in steady
state.
Increases in the stickiness of wages or prices also increases the long-run NAIRU. If firms are less
able to raise wages in response to positive shocks, they will set higher real wages in steady state,

which increases steady-state unemployment. If prices are stickier, then nominal wages do not need
to decline as much in response to negative demand shocks, which means that downward nominal
wage rigidities bind less often. Again, firms will set higher real wages in steady state and employ
fewer workers.
This results may be affected by the calibration of the model. For example, a greater variance in
productivity shocks compared to demand shocks could change how parameters affect the long-run

NAIRU.
5. Conclusion
We construct a model of the labour-market that can generate the asymmetric dynamics of
unemployment in the data (given symmetric shocks) and can be used to study the implications for
inflation. We find that endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder, downward nominal wage rigidity,
and convex recruiting costs are the key frictions that produce the asymmetric dynamics. The model
can generate a substantially positive skewness in unemployment changes, which is a feature of the

data but not most existing structural models of the labour market. The model implies that the short-
run Phillips curve is nonlinear and state-dependent, and that the short-run NAIRU fluctuates cyclically

### Page 22

19
and asymmetrically, tending to increase when unemployment is above trend, but may not decline
when unemployment is below trend. The long-run NAIRU is affected by cyclical factors (e.g. shock
volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime), because the nonlinear cyclical dynamics and aggregate
risk interact to affect firms’ expectations and employment decisions even in the absence of shocks.

A.2 Calibration
Labour flows and stocks: In steady state, hires from unemployment equal quits to unemployment
and layoffs:
Hu = ( Qu + ˜F s) N + Gδ uHu.
Therefore:
Hu = Qu + ˜F s
1 − Gδ u N
EU = Qu + ˜F s
1 − Gδ u
We set G = 0.8 and δ u = 1. Then we set steady state values for N, Qu, and ˜F s so that the stochastic
steady states for N, UE, and EU match their values in the data (somewhere between 2019 values
and 2025 values, which means around 4-4.5% unemployment, 20-25% UE rate, and 1% EU rate).

We assume the quit-to-unemployment rate is around 0.2%, so the layoff rate is around 0.8%, and
the secure layoff rate is around 0.05%.17
We set the steady-state EE rate to try to match its value in the data (around 1-1.5%).
Match productivity distributions: We set δ = 0.1, so that 10% of employees draw a new
productivity each month (and can be laid off). Setting δ too high means that match productivities
are not very persistent and so have little effect on expected match surplus. Setting δ too low means

that layoffs cannot rise much.
We assume that firms do not know the productivity of unemployed matches, so they accept all of
them (F u = 0).
The match productivity distributions F, F u and G are all truncated lognormal. We set the mean of the
main distribution µx = 0.1 and the mean of the distribution for unemployed matches µx,u = −0.5.
The wage of newly hired unemployed workers is a fixed amount lower than the aggregate wage:
Wt −W u t = ξ = 0.5. The standard deviation of the distribution that new hires out of unemployment

draw from after their first period, G, is set to be 3 times larger than the standard deviations of the
main distribution F and the distribution for unemployed matches before they are hired F u.18 The
remaining parameters of the distributions (i.e. the truncation point, the standard deviation, and the
mean of G) are then determined by the steady-state flows.
Matching function and recruiting costs: The matching function elasticity α = 0.5. The convexity

parameter for recruiting costs is set χ = 4. We assume no training cost, κ T = 0, and then κ and κ v
are calibrated so that 20% of total recruiting costs are per-vacancy, while 80% are per-match.
Note that C ˜N,t+1 = −χ
Ct+1
˜Nt and (1 + χ) Ct Mt = CV,t qt + CM,t = He t Mt λ N t + Hu t Mt λ Hu t , so C ˜N,t+1 =
− χ
1+χ
He t+1λ N t+1+Hu t+1λ Hu t+1
˜Nt .
17 Assuming a lot of insecure layoffs and few secure layoffs in steady state (i.e. high G) helps to generate skew in

unemployment changes and slow recoveries. In the Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson model, the exogenous separation
rate for insecurely-attached workers is 96%, compared to 0.02% for securely-attached workers. They say this is the
order of magnitude required to generate slow recoveries.
18 A higher variance for G seems to help generate skewness in unemployment changes.

W N = ε He Heλ N + ε Hu Huλ Hu W W − ξ − ε QQ(1 − δ F) ˜Nλ N + ε QQ(1 − δ ) ˜N(xa − ˜L ˜N )λ L

As γ → ∞ (i.e. idiosyncratic preferences become unimportant), recruitment and separation elasticities
become infinite, and W approaches a weighted average of xa, xa,u and xa,m, while λ N converges to
slightly above 0 (if λ Hu = λ N ), then λ N → 0). As γ → 0 (i.e. idiosyncratic preferences entirely
determine job choices), recruitment and separation elasticities go to zero, and W → 0. The wage
markdown expression differs from the standard ε

1+ε because the elasticities are multiplied by S
1−β
(
1− 1 1+χ S ) . This is because the elasticity applies only to hires in a given period (S), but firms
account for the surplus over the expected lifetime of the match.
We set γ = 100, so the wage markdown is very small. This helps to generate sufficiently large
responses in unemployment, especially in layoffs.19
For wage adjustment costs, we need to choose a function ϕ w(Πw). We set ϕ w to 2500 when nominal

wages growth is above ¯Πw = (1/1.005)(1/12) (i.e. 0.5 percent below steady-state inflation) and
20000 when it is below ¯Πw.
Price setting: We set θ = 7.87 (a standard value in the New Keynesian literature). Price stickiness
ϕ p = 2500.
Policy: We set γ p = 1 48 , which implies that policymakers place four times as much weight on
annualised inflation compared to unemployment in their policy response.
Shocks: The shocks are AR(1) processes with persistence 0.96. The standard deviations are scaled

so that the variance of unemployment matches its variance in the data.
A.3 Solution method
The state variables are ˜Nt−1, Wt−1, ˜Lt−1 and the exogenous shocks At and Mt .
We use policy function iteration to solve for the equilibrium. We approximate λ
˜N
t , λ
˜L
t , Φ′ t Πt , Ψt Πw t
and E p t = IEt Φ′ t+1Πt+1 Yt+1 Yt are piecewise linear functions of the state.
Ideally, we would like to choose variables that can be well-approximated as piecewise linear functions

of the state. This is why we choose to approximate e.g. Ψt Πw t instead of Πw t . Expectations would work
19 Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson similarly have only a very small wage markdown. It is well known in the literature that
search and matching models can struggle to match unemployment data unless match surplus is very small.

### Page 27

24
well, because averaging over possible future states ‘smoothes out’ nonlinearities. The expectations
in the model are λ
˜N
t , λ
˜L
t and
E p t = IEt Φ′ t+1Πt+1 Yt+1 Yt
Ew t = IEt Ψ′ t+1ΠW t+1 Wt+1Nt+1 Wt Nt .
Given values for these variables, all period t variables are determined. But solving the intratemporal
equilibrium conditions to get period t variables requires solving a complicated system of nonlinear
equations. So we have chosen variables which mostly allow us to solve the intratemporal conditions

directly.20
Similarly, we could also instead just approximate four variables, which would likely require fewer
iterations and could be more accurate. But approximating five makes it easier to solve the
intratemporal equilibrium conditions, so each iteration is faster.
20 There is one equation that we cannot solve directly. To avoid using a nonlinear solver within each iteration, we first solve
it on a grid before starting the policy function iteration. Then we use this approximate solution in each iteration.

## Document 398: RBAは２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 ～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念_d8ef4396

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBAは２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 ～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念_d8ef4396.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 4 日

1/6 
RBA は２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 
～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念や追加利上げ期待の後退が豪ドルの重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日に開催した定例会合において、政策金
利を 25bp 引き上げて 4.85％とすることを決定した。利上げは２会合連続、年明け以降５回
目となり、政策金利は18 年ぶりの高水準となった。 
○ 背景には、原油高に加え、国内の需給ひっ迫を反映してインフレ圧力が根強く、物価上昇が
幅広い財・サービスに波及していることがある。ＲＢＡは物価見通しを大幅に上方修正し、
インフレ率が目標レンジに収束する時期も従来の想定から後ずれすると見込んでいる。 
○ 一方で、今回の利上げ決定においては、理事９人のうち３人が据え置きを支持するなど、景
気減速への警戒感も強まっている。ブロック総裁は、これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響
を見極める必要性を強調しており、ＲＢＡは当面、政策金利を現行水準に据え置き、物価や
雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。ただし、インフレ圧力が高止
まりすれば追加利上げに動く可能性も残ると考えられる。 
○ 豪ドル相場については、利上げが事前に織り込まれていたことに加え、追加利上げ期待の後
退を受けて弱含んだ。先行きについても、国内景気の減速懸念や米国の金融政策、中国景気、
中東情勢などの影響を受けつつ、当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/6 
【RBAは２会合連続の利上げ決定、年明け以降５回目、政策金利は 18 年ぶりの高水準に】 
オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日の日程で開催した定例の金融政策委員会に
おいて、政策金利であるオフィシャルキ
ャッシュレート（ＯＣＲ）を 25bp 引き上
げて 4.85％とすることを決定した（図
１）。ＲＢＡは、２月の定例会合で２年３
ヶ月ぶりの利上げに動き、５月の定例会
合まで３会合連続の利上げを実施した。
その後、６月会合、８月会合では金利を
据え置く様子見姿勢に転じたものの、９
月会合で３会合ぶりの利上げに動いた。
このため、今回は２会合連続、年明け以
降５回目となる。今回の利上げにより、
ＯＣＲは18 年ぶりの水準となった。 
事前の市場予想では、ＲＢＡが利上げに動くとの見方が大宗を占めた。背景には、ＲＢＡ高官か
ら先行きの政策運営について、タカ派姿勢を維持する発言が相次いだことがある。なかでもハウザ
ー副総裁は、原油高などを理由にインフレ見通しが「著しく悪化している」として、金融政策では
供給ショックに対処できないとしつつ、コスト上昇がインフレ期待の上昇に波及することを防ぐ必
要があるとの考えを示した。そのうえ、物価動向がＲＢＡの想定を上回る可能性が高いとの見方を
示すなど、インフレに対する警戒感を強調した。同氏の発言は政策運営の方向性を明示したもので
はないものの、インフレ見通しの悪化に加え、インフレ期待を抑え込む必要性を強調したことは、
金融市場に追加利上げを意識させるに十分であったと考えられる（注１）。

注１ 10 月21 日付レポート「弱含む動きをみせてきた豪ドル相場は持ち直しに向かうか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666763.html）

７-９月のインフレ率は前年同期比＋3.9％と前期（同＋3.9％）から横ばいで推移し、ＲＢＡが定
める目標レンジ（２～３％）を引き続き上回るとともに、ＲＢＡが８月会合で示した見通し（前年
比＋3.6％）をも上回った（図２）。前期
比も＋1.3％と前期（同＋0.6％）から上
昇ペースが加速し、食料品やエネルギー
など生活必需品の物価上昇に加え、サー
ビス物価も上昇基調を強める動きが確

値）も前年同期比＋3.7％と前期（同＋
3.6％）からわずかに伸びが加速してお
り、ＲＢＡの見通し（同＋3.3％）も上回
っ た。前期 比も＋1.0％ と前期（ 同＋
0.8％）から上昇ペースが加速しており、
基調的なインフレ圧力が強まっていることがうかがえた。さらに、ＲＢＡのブロック総裁が議会証
言において、これまでの利上げにより物価が目標に回帰させることが可能か、今回会合で議論する
考えを示すとともに、物価上昇圧力が依然強いとの認識を示した。このため、金融市場では利上げ
が実施されるとの見方が一段と強まった（注２）。

【評決は６対３に割れる、インフレ見通しを上方修正する一方、景気の下振れリスクにも警戒】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、理事９人のうち６人が利上げに賛成した一方、３人は据え置き
を支持するなど、金融引き締めの必要性を巡って判断が分かれたことが明らかにされた。前回の９
月会合では、全会一致で利上げを決定したことを踏まえれば、今回の票割れは、インフレ抑制を優
先する姿勢が維持される一方、景気減速への警戒が理事会内で強まりつつあることを示唆している。 
物価動向について「依然高水準にある」、「足元のデータはインフレの広がりを示唆している」と
の認識を示した。加えて、「インフレ見通しも大幅に上方修正された」として、その理由に「イラン
情勢の悪化による燃料やその他商品市況の上昇を一部反映した」ことを挙げた。一方で、「同国内に
おける供給能力のひっ迫も想定以上にやや厳しい状況にある」との見方を示し、「 ＡＩ（人工知能）
関連投資が技術関連のインフレ圧力を招いているほか、多くの財・サービスでインフレ圧力が依然
高い」、「多くの企業がコスト上昇圧力に直面するなか、財・サービス価格をすでに引き上げ、ない
し検討を行っており、インフレ率は 2027 年を通じて高止まりすると予想している」とした。その
結果、「インフレ率が目標レンジに達するのは2027 年後半と従来の想定から約３四半期遅れる」と
して、「インフレ期待への上振れリスクをもたらしている」との見方を示した。 
会合にあわせて公表した最新の『四半期金融政策報告』では、物価見通しが大幅に上方修正され

注２ 10 月28 日付レポート「豪ドル相場に持ち直しの兆し、RBA による追加利上げ観測が再燃」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666938.html）

### Page 4

4/6 
た。2026 年末のインフレ率は前年同期比＋4.5％と、８月会合時点の見通し（同＋3.6％）から大幅
に上方修正された。コアインフレ率についても、2027 年半ばの見通しを前年同期比＋3.5％と、８
月会合時点（同＋3.0％）から上方修正した。そのうえで、ＲＢＡはインフレ率が目標レンジの上限
である３％以下となる時期を 2027 年末近くまで後ずれすると見込んでおり、目標レンジの中央値
である 2.5％への収束も2028 年半ばになるとの見方を示している。加えて、原油高などに伴う企業
のコスト増加が販売価格や賃金設定に波及し、家計や企業のインフレ期待が高止まりするリスクに
も警戒感を示した。 
一方、金融環境については「年明け以降の利上げにより引き締まった」との見方を示した。その
うえで、「経済は減速し、不動産価格も大幅に下落し、失業率も上昇している」として、先行きは
「向こう１年で生産の伸びは一段と鈍化が見込まれ、生産能力への圧力が緩和され、インフレ率を
目標レンジに戻す一助になることが期待される」とした。また、「金利上昇や不動産価格・取引高の
下落は、不動産投資や家計消費を下押しする」とする一方、「データセンター関連投資を含む、企業
部門による設備投資は当面堅調な推移が見込まれ」、「雇用も増加が見込まれるが、失業率は８月会
合時点の想定より大幅に上昇する」との見通しを示した。 
ただし、実体経済や物価見通しについては「依然として不確実性が高い」として、「イラン情勢の
行方は見通せず、インフレ率が想定を上回り、実体経済が想定を下回るシナリオも考えられる」と
し、「 不確実性が長期化すれば、世界経済や同国の経済成長率が低下する可能性がある」とした。一
方で、「足元ではＡＩ関連投資による押し上げ効果がイラン情勢の悪影響を上回っており、主要貿
易相手国の景気は想定を上回る力強さが続いている」ものの、「同国では生産性の伸びの鈍化が潜
在成長率の重しとなり、不動産市場の低迷の度合いや実体経済への影響にも不確実性が残る」との
見方を示した。 
今回の決定については「インフレ期待が高止まりするリスクが高まっている」ほか、「インフレ見
通しや上振れリスクを踏まえ、目標レンジに戻すためにはさらなる引き締めが妥当と判断した」と
した。そのうえで、先行きも「インフレ率を持続的に目標レンジに戻すために必要な措置を引き続
き講じる」として、「経済指標や見通し、リスクに関する評価を注視する」としつつ、「政策対応は
情勢変化に対応できる態勢が整っており、物価安定と完全雇用の実現というマンデートに注力する」
との考えを示した。ただし、９月会合では追加利上げに言及する文言が含まれていたものの、今回
はそうした文言は削除されるなど明確な方向感を示されなかった。

【ブロック総裁は過去の利上げ効果を見極める姿勢を強調、追加利上げには慎重な構えをみせる】 
一方で、今回の会合後に実施されたブロック総裁の記者会見では、先行きの金融政策運営につい
て慎重な姿勢がうかがわれた。同氏は、今回の利上げ決定について、原油価格の上昇による直接的
なインフレへの対応ではなく、従来からの国内における需給ひっ迫や、エネルギー価格の上昇が幅
広い物価に波及する二次的な影響を抑制することへの対応と説明した。ただし、年明け以降に実施
した５回の利上げのうち、直近の利上げの効果は実体経済に十分に浸透していないとして、今後公
表される経済指標を見極める必要性を強調した。今回の会合で据え置きを支持した理事についても、

### Page 5

5/6 
不動産市場の調整や失業率の上昇、賃金上昇率が加速していないことなどを重視し、金融引き締め
の効果を確認するために時間を置くべきだと判断したことを明らかにした。 
事実、足元の国内景気にはこれまでの金融引き締めの影響が広がりをみせている。９月の失業率
は 4.7％と上昇傾向にあるとともに、不動産価格の下落や住宅ローン需要が減少していることなど、
金利上昇に敏感な分野で調整圧力が強まる動きが確認されている。ＲＢＡは今回公表した最新の経
済見通しにおいて、2027 年末の実質 GDP 成長率を前年同期比＋1.3％と、８月会合時点の見通し（同
＋1.6％）から下方修正している。さらに、失業率も 2028 年末に 5.1％まで上昇すると予想してお
り、８月会合時点の見通し（4.8％）から上方修正している。同氏は、中心的な見通しでは景気後退
を想定していないとしつつ、経済成長率の大幅な鈍化や家計消費の弱さが続くとの認識を示した。 
注目されるのは、前述した通りＲＢＡが声明文から、必要に応じて追加利上げを行うとの明示的
な表現を削除したことである。同氏は、これを金融政策の方向転換を示すものと受け止めるべきで
はないと説明しており、追加利上げの可能性を排除してはいない。ただし、理事会内において判断
が分かれたことに加え、これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響を考慮すれば、ＲＢＡが今後も断続
的に利上げを実施する可能性は低下したと考えられる。当面は現行水準で政策金利を据え置き、物
価や雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。一方で、原油価格の高止まりや
インフレ期待の上昇が続けば、2027 年前半に追加利上げに動く可能性も残る。反対に、不動産市場
の調整が深刻化するとともに、個人消費や雇用の悪化が想定以上に進む場合には、金融引き締めの
長期化を見直す必要性が強まるとみられる。

【豪ドル相場は利上げ決定後に弱含む動き、先行きも当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性】 
金融市場では、前述の通り今回の利上げが事前に相当程度織り込まれていたこともあり、決定直
後の豪ドル相場は対米ドルで下落した
（図３）。声明において、追加利上げを明

さらに、豪ドル相場は国内の金融政策だけでなく、米国の金融政策や中国景気、国際商品市況、
世界的な投資家のリスク選好の影響を受けやすい。仮にＲＢＡが追加利上げに踏み切る一方、米国

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary: Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid-term elections. The near-term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive, the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike: Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm. Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far. Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all-time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 18.2, still well 
under the 25–30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September. Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negative (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks? We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25–30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid-term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings: 
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable. We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike: The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10-year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near-term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10-year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish. Unsurprisingly, the market-
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes by June 
2027. A December rate hike now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would 
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre-existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

The US Dollar Index (DXY) is likely to stay 
rangebound between 102 and 103, supported by 
elevated US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year-on-year (y/y), but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline, supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge-off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute. However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq-100’s valuation de-rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

US banks’ revenues remain resilient despite 
moderation from a record-high Q2 2026

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agency rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12-18 months, agencies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact. While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation 
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support. While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks include rising deposit costs, mark-to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2. Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures and improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break above this level would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated. We maintain our three- and 12-month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure. 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure, with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September at only around 60% of pre-
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

### Page 2

The Week Ahead November 20, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Korea (November 26)

We expect the BOK to raise its policy 
rate by 25bp to 3.25% at its November 
meeting, although the decision is likely 
to be close. The BOK's October minutes 
showed that most board members 
continued to favour further tightening. 
Recent economic indicators, including 
exports and PMIs, have remained solid, 
while CPI inflation stayed elevated at 2.8% 
yoy, above the BOK's 2% target.

That said, equity market volatility has 
increased amid concerns over AI 
financing risks, potential US AI 
regulatory constraints and intensifying 
US-China technology rivalry. These 
uncertainties could weigh on business 
confidence and domestic consumption, 
adding to the case for caution. 
Nevertheless, we think the BOK will 
place greater weight on domestic 
economic fundamentals than on short-
term market volatility, and continue to 
lean towards a 25bp hike.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s inflation to 
remain contained in October 2026, with 
headline and core inflation easing to 1.9% 
yoy and 1.7% yoy, respectively, from 2.2% 
yoy and 2.0% yoy in September, as 
favourable base effects kicked in. The 
moderation reflected smaller positive 
contributions from electricity & gas and 
retail & other goods, contained services 
inflation, and a slower increase in private 
transport costs as car price growth 
eased, despite a slight uptick in food 
inflation.

We expect industrial production to 
moderate to 1.0% yoy in October 2026, 
from 3.4% yoy in September, reflecting 
high base effects after output surged by 
15.0% yoy in the same period last year, 
the strongest monthly growth in 2025. 
Beneath the softer headline, robust 
artificial intelligence-related demand 
likely underpinned precision engineering 
and electronics production, although 
electronics growth may have moderated. 
Biomedical manufacturing likely also 
provided support, consistent with the 
sharp 253.1% yoy rise in pharmaceutical 
exports, despite unfavourable base 
effects.

We anticipate that 3Q26 final GDP 
growth will be revised higher to 5.6% yoy 
and 1.3% qoq sa, from advance estimates 
of 5.4% yoy and 1.1% qoq sa, mainly 
reflecting stronger-than-initially-
reported manufacturing expansion. 
Attention will shift to the 2027 outlook, 
where we expect the government to 
forecast growth of 2.5-4.5%.

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to remain 
robust at 61.8% yoy in October, 
supported by China’s strong export 
growth of 35.2% and continued demand 
for AI-related electronics. Broad-based 
strength across Asian markets should 
further support Hong Kong’s re-export 
trade. Import growth is also expected to

### Page 3

The Week Ahead November 20, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

stay firm, underpinned by resilient 
export orders and improving investment 
sentiment, which continue to support 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

## Document 425: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

2 The industries are: (1) Basic Materials, (2), Consumer Discretionary, (3), Consumer Staples, (4) Energy, (5) 
Financials, (6) Health Care, (7) Industrials, (9) Real Estate, (10) Technology, (11) Telecommunications, (12) 
Utilities. 
3 Q4 2025 saw a large increase in the AI use score for the “Technology” industry, jumping up to around 6. This 
one-off spike was driven by ASML’s announcement of a significant strategic partnership with and investment 
in Mistral AI” to 'embed AI across our entire holistic portfolio in order to increase the performance and 
productivity of our systems and the yield of our customers' processes'. Because of ASML’s large market cap 
(around 49% of our Technology category), it distorts the weighted average significantly. 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use 
scores than other sectors for much of the sample, utilities converged 
towards the STOXX 600 average during 2026. The result shows that the 
rise in reported adoption extends beyond the sectors most closely 
associated with AI.

Figure 3: AI use score (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The value for each industry is computed as a weighted average of all firms within the industry where the weights are derived from the current 
STOXX 600 share of each company. The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.  
11 November 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

AI-use scores rise across country groups, with indications of faster growth from 
2025. The shared direction suggests a broad European listed-company trend, 
even though differences in score levels and timing persist between countries.

Figure 4: AI use intensity (0-10) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a geographical cross-check, our index matches the broad trends of the 
Eurostat ICT annual survey, which places Germany as a clear frontrunner among 
its large peers. But some differences do exist. For instance, Eurostat shows the 
Netherlands with a very high AI adoption. As Figure 5 demonstrates, there is a 
positive correlation between the AI use score metric derived here, and that from 
Eurostat, even though the Eurostat data measures if firms are adopting AI, and 
our new use measure also incorporates the concept of how intensively firms are 
using AI.

Figure 5: Relationship between Eurostat usage and Weighted share of STOXX 
600 mentioning AI usage

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Eurostat. Notes: each dot represents a country-year pair of AI usage>0

AI productivity impact: Reported benefits are rising, but 
transformational gains remain uncommon 
Higher intensity of use does not necessarily mean firms are benefiting more from 
AI. But our AI productivity impact metric does show a similar pattern to our AI 
usage metric, although it tends to be slightly lower, indicating that companies 
may not yet be reaping the full rewards for their AI investment. 
Financials stand out more clearly on reported productivity benefits than on AI-use 
intensity, while German companies again record relatively strong scores, but only 
in more recent history.

Figure 6: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

AI productivity impact across European industries 
Reported AI-productivity benefits have become more prominent across 
industries. Figure 6 shows scores rising gradually during 2022–25, followed by 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 9

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

indications of a faster increase from late 2025. The profile resembles the AI-use 
trend in Figure 4, suggesting that the stronger adoption narrative is accompanied 
by more discussion of operational gains. 
The aggregate trajectories of the AI use and AI productivity impact scores are 
similar, but sector rankings differ. Technology’s early relative strength in AI use is 
not matched by its productivity score, while financials report above-average 
productivity benefits despite more modest use scores. 
AI productivity impact across countries 
Figure 7 plots the self-report impact of AI on company productivity by country. 
Between 2022-23, German companies recorded average productivity impact, 
even though their AI use score was relatively high. However more recently, their 
AI impact score has persistently been above average. The UK grouping shows 
persistent early outperformance in AI impact during 2022–23, followed by 
convergence towards the aggregate average.

Figure 7: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

Figure 8 shows that there is a strong positive correlation between the AI use score 
and the AI productivity score across the STOXX 600 (r-squared = 0.83). The 
relationship between the scores is approximately linear, with a slope of around 
11 November 2026

Deutsche Bank AG Page 10

0.66: a one-point higher use score is associated with roughly a 0.66-point higher 
productivity score for each country-year.

Figure 8: Productivity and use scores are correlated, but less than 1:1

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

AI labour force impact: On net, AI may be benefiting 
employment, for now… 
Across industries and countries, the recorded score for AI’s impact on the labour 
force remains relatively small. On average, the reported impact on companies’ 
employment has been fairly neutral, with a marginal negative bias over the last 
year or so.

Figure 9: AI's impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 11

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale. 
AI labour force impact across countries 
Workforce-impact scores are also small across the main country groupings, with 
most countries slowing a slight negative tilt in recent years (Figure 10).

Figure 10: AI impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

The lack of significant impact so far from AI on companies’ labour forces so far is 
consistent with results from our previous work looking at the other side of the 
labour market. Data from our dbDataInsights household survey showed that 
workers across Europe reported a fairly low level of fear about losing their job to 
AI over the next year. The analysis in that report found results that were more 
consistent with workers adapting to the new AI world by upskilling and 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 12

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

demanding higher wages. Those results would not be consistent with a world in 
which AI was actively replacing labour. 
These findings of a low impact on labour are highly consistent with recent 
academic research (e.g., Yotzov et al., 2026), which found that nearly half of 
surveyed UK and German firms reported absolutely no impact on headcount from 
AI over the past three years, 
Conclusion 
The newly developed framework provides a recurring way to monitor company 
disclosures about AI’s impact on productivity and labour. The central finding of 
this report is a broadening AI transition rather than clear evidence of an 
economy-wide productivity breakthrough. The new metrics indicate that AI use 
remains limited but is rising across the European countries and industries 
represented in the sample. Companies report low but growing productivity gains 
and little aggregate workforce impact so far. This pattern is consistent with a 
technology J-curve, with firms still integrating the new technology, rather than 
proof that this trajectory will unfold. Limited reported workforce effects today do 
not rule out subsequent layoffs or wider organisational restructuring. 
Quarterly updates to this framework will track Europe’s AI transition and provide 
timely signals of whether companies are reporting more substantial productivity 
and employment effects. 
Beyond AI, the framework could be adapted to extract signals about employment 
decisions, investment decisions and the general economic outlook from earnings 
calls. These potential extensions could form the basis of alternative nowcasting 
models for important macroeconomic data; they are applications that will be 
explored in future work.

## Document 434: US SECTORS CALL_ Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples
Date:    5 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Financials, Health Care & Consumer Staples

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL: Financials,
Health Care & Consumer
Staples
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 15 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Stocks bounced back last week after the prior week’s AI selloff. The S&P
500 rose 2.5% and is up 10.5% ytd. The chip trade rebounded, but
leadership changed hands. Nvidia’s board opened an investigation into
allegations against CEO Jensen Huang, who resigned on Thursday. CFO
Colette Kress is interim CEO. Investors moved into the stocks of Nvidia’s
rivals. AMD rose 19% for the week, and Broadcom rose 18% as Nvidia fell
13%.
Health Care and Financials have outperformed since the market’s March

30 low. Health Care is up 31.1% and Financials 21.1%, both ahead of the
S&P 500’s 19.2% (chart). Staples has lagged, up just 12.3% over the
same period, even though it's up 20.2% ytd.
Here’s more on Financials, Health Care, and Consumer Staples:
(1) Financials. The IPO window is wide open. SpaceX raised $75 billion
in June, the largest IPO ever, and its shares rose 19% on the first day.
Anthropic’s shares are expected to begin trading on Thursday at $70-$78

each, implying a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to $1.40 trillion. Its
S-1 filing affirmed that AI customers will pay up for compute. In Q2,
Anthropic earned $2.08 of revenue per dollar of compute and
infrastructure expense, up fivefold from $0.40 in Q1-2025 (in Q3, however,
the figure dipped to $1.72, as the company bought costlier short-term
capacity to meet demand). Big AI listings bring fees for underwriters,
trading desks, and the asset managers who buy the deals (chart).

### Page 3

Analysts have expected a strong year for capital markets. They
collectively forecast Investment Banking & Brokerage revenues to grow
21.1% this year, up from 7.4% in December (chart). Earnings are forecast
to grow 30.9%. Yet the industry’s forward P/E has fallen to 13.6 from 19.1
earlier this year. Investors are not paying for the deal boom.
The Financials sector’s forward profit margin is a record 22.1%, and the
profitability is broad-based. Forward profit margins are at or near record

highs across the sector’s biggest industries: Asset Management &
Custody Banks (28.6%), Regional Banks (28.5%), Investment Banking
(28.1%), and Diversified Banks (27.8%) (chart).

### Page 4

The Financials sector’s forward P/E has fallen to 15.2 from 16.4 at year-
end 2025, while its forward earnings is up 14.0% ytd. The sector has been
getting cheaper as it gets more profitable. We retain our overweight rating
on Financials.
(2) Health Care. Health Care has been the best-performing sector since
March 30. It is up 21.8% ytd. Forward earnings is up 11.5% ytd, and the
forward P/E is up 9.4% (chart). It trades at 19.8 times forward earnings, a

premium to the S&P 500’s 18.2.
Health Care’s profitability has been turning up. The sector’s forward profit
margin has risen to 8.7% from a low of 7.9% in January (chart). Within the
sector, the Biotechnology industry’s forward profit margin is at 33.4% and
Pharmaceuticals’ is at 31.1%; both have been climbing.

### Page 5

The Pharmaceuticals industry accounts for 37% of the sector’s market
capitalization. Analysts collectively expect Pharma earnings to fall 5.9%
this year, then jump 29.6% in 2027 (chart). But they’ve more than doubled
their revenue sights this year: The consensus 2026 revenue estimate
implies growth of 9.9%, up from 4.0% in December. The rebound rests on
sales, not just a low base from which the growth is measured.
AI should speed progress up in the medical world. In Anthropic’s IPO

roadshow video on Tuesday, CEO Dario Amodei claimed that AI can
compress 50 to 100 years of medical research into five to ten. We think
the benefits to drug discovery could be huge.
Investors already see it. Moderna is up 623.8% ytd following the success
of its personalized cancer vaccine, designed with the help of machine

### Page 6

learning. Clinical trials and approvals still take years, so the payoff will
build over time. Pharma’s 2027 rebound doesn’t depend on it. We
continue to recommend overweighting Health Care.
(3) Consumer Staples. Staples is one of only three sectors that got more
expensive this year. Its forward P/E has risen to 23.9 from 20.8 at the end
of 2025. Over the same period, the S&P 500’s P/E fell to 18.2 from 22.2,
and IT’s fell to 17.2 from 26.5 (chart). Only Real Estate and Consumer

Discretionary trade at higher multiples.
Forward earnings is up just 4.6% ytd, while the forward P/E is up 13.5%
(chart). Almost all of that re-rating came after late September, when
investors fled the AI trade for defensive stocks.

### Page 7

Profitability has not improved. The sector’s forward profit margin is 7.1%,
below its 2021 peak of 7.7% (chart). Analysts have cut their 2027 earnings
growth estimate to 5.6% from 7.3% in August. Investors are paying 23.9
times forward earnings for single-digit-percentage growth.
Walmart and Target are set to report quarterly earnings next week. The
Merchandise Retail industry trades at 36.4 times forward earnings, its
highest forward P/E since earlier this year (chart). Investors pay up for

Consumer Staples stocks when they fear an economic slowdown. But the
labor market is picking up, and consumers are spending. That makes the
valuation premium characteristic of defensive sectors hard to justify. We
retain our underweight rating on Consumer Staples.

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 524: 中国製ハイブリッド車の輸入制限に踏み切るEU

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国製ハイブリッド車の輸入制限に踏み切るEU.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 20 日

MURC Focus

中国製ハイブリッド車の輸入制限に踏み切る EU

～日本にとっても“対岸の火事”ではない～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 12 月にセーフガードを発動 
欧州連合（EU）は 10 月 12 日、輸入が急増している中国製のハイブリッド車（HV）とプラグインハイブリッド車
（PHV）を対象に、12 月よりセーフガード（緊急輸入制限）を発動すると発表した。期間は 4 年間で、1 年目は HV
と PHV の年間輸入台数を計 35 万台とし、その後の増加幅は年間 6%までに制限する。EU 籍のメーカーが中
国で生産する車も対象となるが、中国メーカーの EU 域内での生産車は対象外となる。

EU は 2024 年 10 月 30 日より、中国製 EV（電気自動車）に対し、基本関税 10%に加えて、最大で 35.3%の
追加関税を課している。廉価な中国製 EV の輸入を抑制する目的からだが、一方で HV と PHV には追加関税
を課してこなかった。その結果、中国製 HV と PHV の輸入が 2025 年から急増し、EU 製の HV と PHV は劣勢
に追いやられた（図表 1）。また電動車の対中貿易収支も、HV と PHV を中心に赤字が膨らんだ（図表 2）。 
図表 1. 中国製電動車の輸入台数の推移 図表 2. 電動車の対中貿易収支

（注）EV:電気自動車、PHV:プラグインハイブリッド車、HV: ハイブリッド車 
（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット）

（注）EV:電気自動車、PHV:プラグインハイブリッド車、HV: ハイブリッド車

（億ユーロ）
〇EU は10月12日、輸入が急増している中国製の HV と PHV を対象に、12月よりセーフガード（緊急輸入制
限）を発動すると発表した。 
〇2025年に再登板した米国のドナルド・トランプ大統領による貿易・関税政策を保護主義だと批判する EU だ
が、その EU もまた保護主義の性格を強めている。 
〇今回の中国製 HV と PHV に対するセーフガードは、日本にとって“対岸の火事”ではない。政府と日系メーカ
ーが一丸となり、EU に対して互恵主義に基づく平等な競争条件の順守を訴えていくことが望まれる。

### Page 2

EU の 2025 年の中国製 HV と PHV の輸入台数の実績値は計 30 万台であったが、2026 年の輸入台数は
80 万台に上ると予測されている（1～7 月期の実績値は約 38 万台）。通商担当であるマロシュ・シェフチョビッチ
欧州委員は今回の措置で、2027 年の中国製 HV と PHV の輸入台数（計 35 万台）は 2026 年から半減すると
アピールしている。

とはいえ EU は、中国製 HV と PHV の輸入台数が年間 6%まで増加することを容認しているため、2027 年の
輸入台数が計 35 万台に抑制されても、セーフガード最終年の 2030 年の輸入台数は計 42 万台まで容認するこ
とになる。要するに EU は、中国との全面的な対立を回避するために、EU 市場に対する一定のマーケットアクセ
スを容認していることになる。

2. 特に中国に対して保護主義傾向を強める EU 
このように、2025 年に再登板した米国のドナルド・トランプ大統領による貿易・関税政策を保護主義だと批判す
る EU もまた、保護主義の性格を強めているが、背景には国際競争力の低下がある。一例が価格競争力の指標
である実質実効為替レートで、人民元と日本円とで比較すると、ユーロの増価が鮮明である（図表 3）。これは欧
州中銀（ECB）による高金利政策のみならず、EU の高インフレに伴う内外価格差の拡大に起因する。

高インフレが生じた理由は、そのエネルギー政策（ロシア産の安価なエネルギーの排除とそれに伴う再生可能
エネルギーへのシフト）に加えて、賃金政策（最低賃金の引上げなどに起因する物価・賃金スパイラル）の影響に
よるところが大きい。それ以外にも、EU の執行部局である欧州委員会が環境面を中心に、いわゆる“過剰規制”
を課したことが産業競争力の低下を招いたと指摘される。

図表 3. 実質実効為替レートの比較 図表 4. EU の貿易収支

### Page 3

競争力を失った EU の貿易収支は安定した黒字を稼ぐことができなくなり、直近 2026 年 4～6 月期は▲220
億ユーロと、イラン発のエネルギーショックに伴うエネルギー価格高騰のあおりもあって 2023 年 1～3 月期以来
の赤字になった（図表 4）。特に EU が抱える対中貿易赤字は巨額であり、EU はその削減を重視している。今回
の中国製 HV と PHV に対するセーフガードの発動も、こうした動きの延長線上にある。

EU は雇用確保の観点から、中国企業に対して、輸出を増やすのではなく EU 域内で生産をするように求めて
いる。そのための直接投資（FDI）の受け入れに EU は積極的だが、同時に EU は防衛やインフラなど戦略産業
に関しては中国による投資や中国製品を排除する意向を鮮明にしている。いずれにせよ、米国に比べれば穏や
かとはいえ、EU もまた、とりわけ中国を念頭に保護主義の動きを強めている。

3. 日本も EU の保護主義対象に 
今回の EU の措置は、あくまで中国製 HV と PHV に対するものである。そのため、他国製 HV と PHV には
適用されない。日系自動車メーカーもまた日本から、あるいはトルコの生産拠点などから EU 向けに自動車（主に
HV）を輸出しているが、これらの HV と PHV は今回のセーフガードの対象にならない（図表 5）。ただし、結局の
ところ、EU の保護主義は先行き、日本にも及んでくることが懸念される。

実際、欧州委員会が 2026 年 3 月に提案した産業加速化法（IAA）に基づき、EU が公共調達や公的支援で
域内製品の優遇を図る際には、日本や韓国、トルコなどの友好国から輸入した自動車などはその対象から排除さ
れると報道されている。また日系を含めた非 EU 籍のメーカーが EU 域内で生産した自動車も、EU 域内のメー
カーからの部品調達がなされない限り、同様に IAA の対象から排除される方向にある。

### Page 4

日本と EU は 2019 年 2 月 1 日に経済連携協定（日 EU・EPA）を発効しており、互恵主義に基づく自由で公
正な貿易と投資を尊重し合う関係にある。そして日本も、輸入を中心に対 EU 貿易を拡大してきた（図表 6）。しか
しながら、このように自由主義に基づく通商関係を確認した日本に対しても、EU は結局のところ、保護主義の性
格を強めつつある。

こうした傾向は、ウルズラ・フォン・デア・ライエン欧州委員長を中心とする現体制が続く限りは修正が望みにく
い。このように整理していくと、今回の中国製 HV と PHV に対するセーフガードは、日本にとって “対岸の火事”
ではない。EU が日本に対して不当競争を強いることがないよう、政府と日系メーカーが一丸となり、EU に対して
互恵主義に基づく平等な競争条件の順守を訴えていくことが望まれるところである。

油价与美债收益率的相关性已从 9 ⽉的 65% 降⾄不⾜ 20% ，但 10 年期美债收益率仍⾼于
5% ，英国两年期国债收益率突破 5% 。市场焦点转向通胀黏性与央⾏加息预期。 PGIM

摘要：
全球债市正与油价⾛势脱钩，通胀黏性与央⾏鹰派⽴场重新成为左右国债收益率的关键因素。
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇，据英国《⾦融时报》报道，尽管国际原油价格近期⼤幅回落，美国、英国等主要经
济体的国债收益率却依然居⾼不下。曾主导今年全球债券市场的油价与国债收益率⾼度联动关
系正在⽡解，市场焦点已从能源价格冲击转向各⼤央⾏如何应对持续的通胀压⼒。
美国⽅⾯，美联储 9 ⽉以来重启加息，市场预计下⽉将再次加息。上周，美国国债遭遇逾 18 个⽉
来最差单周表现，强于预期的通胀数据和美联储官员释放的鹰派信号成为主要导⽕索。英国⽅
⾯，尽管布伦特原油价格已从 10 ⽉接近 120 美元 / 桶的⾼位回落，⼀度跌破 90 美元，两年期英国

国债收益率近⽇仍突破 5% ，创三年多新⾼。
资产管理公司 Vanguard 国际利率主管 Ales Koutny 表⽰，" 政府债券已与油价脱钩，回归⾃⾝固
有属性。 "投资机构 PGIM Credit 联席⾸席投资官 Greg Peters 则将当前通胀压⼒⽐作 " 病毒 " ，称
其⼀旦进⼊经济系统便难以清除。
油债联动关系⽡解，市场焦点转向通胀
今年 2 ⽉中东冲突爆发后，油价飙升推动美国国债收益率同步⾛⾼，⼆者之间的滚动相关性⼀度
在 9 ⽉升⾄ 65% ，达到数⼗年来最⾼⽔平。部分债券基⾦经理甚⾄形容，当时⾃⼰ " 基本上只是在
做原油交易员 " 。
然⽽，随着美伊和谈预期升温，国际油价⼤幅回落。美国原油基准西德克萨斯轻质原油（ WTI ）
已从 10 ⽉盘中⾼点 110 美元 / 桶跌⾄不⾜ 90 美元，但 10 年期美国国债收益率仍维持在 5% 上⽅，距
离 2002 年以来的⾼点不远。
据衍⽣品机构 Cboe 分析，油价与国债收益率之间的相关性已从 65% 的峰值降⾄不⾜ 20% 。油价

回落不再带来债券市场的同步缓和，反映出投资者正在重新评估通胀⻛险及其对货币政策的影

推动国债收益率维持⾼位的核⼼因素，已从能源价格转向央⾏政策路径。资产管理公司 Ninety
One 多资产收益主管 John Stopford 指出，近期债券市场⾛弱是 " 对央⾏鹰派⾔论的回应 " ，反映

Chris Waller 表⽰， 12 ⽉加息 50 个基点 " 应当纳⼊考量 " 。叠加强于预期的通胀数据，市场随即加

乎相当⼴泛 " ，同时受到供需两端因素影响，并⽀持连续会议加息。
英国也⾯临类似压⼒。尽管布伦特原油本⽉⼀度跌破 90 美元，两年期英国国债收益率近⽇仍突
破 5% ，创三年多新⾼。利率掉期市场显⽰，英国央⾏下⽉再次加息的概率约为 75% ，并预计明
年还将进⼀步收紧政策。英国央⾏决策者警告，经济韧性与⾼能源价格的溢出效应叠加，可能
需要连续多次加息应对。
通胀压⼒难消，债市避险属性逐步恢复
部分投资者担忧，经历多轮供应冲击后，通胀压⼒正逐渐变得顽固。 PGIM Credit 联席⾸席投资
官 Greg Peters 在接受英国《⾦融时报》 Unhedged 播客采访时表⽰，" 通胀通常是⼀种病毒 ……
⼀旦进⼊系统，便很难根除。 "
Aberdeen Investments 投资总监 Matt Amis 则表⽰，英国央⾏若要对通胀前景更感 " 放⼼ " ，需要
看到 " 中东冲突出现实质性缓和，或油价⻓期维持低位 " 。这意味着，即使能源价格回落，央⾏仍

需要更多证据确认通胀⻛险真正消退。
与此同时，随着油价对债券收益率的直接传导效应减弱，债市与股市之间的传统对冲关系正在
逐步修复。 Vanguard 的 Koutny 表⽰， " 股跌债涨 " 的格局似乎正在重新确⽴，近期每当股市下
挫，美国国债及其他避险政府债券均出现阶段性买盘。
对于全球固定收益市场⽽⾔，原油价格作为债市⻛向标的作⽤正在减弱，通胀数据与央⾏政策
路径重新成为影响收益率⾛势的关键变量。

## Document 578: 凱基投顧_9933 中鼎_賴偉中_20261116

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_9933 中鼎_賴偉中_20261116.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R      Powered by EFA Platform 1

動態更新 工程服務‧台灣

中鼎 
(9933.TW/9933 TT)

毛利率結構性提升，獲利大幅上修 
重要訊息 
中鼎於 11/16 召開法說會。 
評論及分析 
毛利率維持高檔且有高額業外利益，3Q26 財報優於預期。中鼎 3Q26 毛利
率 13.4%、QoQ-0.3ppts，維持今年以來站穩 13%以上的表現，營業利益
季增 7.7%至 24.73 億元，優於凱基預期及市場共識 20.1%、4.1%。業外
有銅衍生商品避險，認列公允價值評價利益 4.3 億元， EPS 2.17 元、
QoQ+32.3%、YoY+173.2%，優於凱基預估及市場共識 49.5%、43.6%。 
高毛利率並非一時，獲利上修。中鼎累計前三季毛利率 13.39%，大幅高於
過去十年平均值之 6.98%，且單季均超過 13%。管理層說明毛利率提升並
非一次性因素，而是 2024 年起接案毛利率提升，且對原物料充分避險，隨
著高毛利專案陸續進入工程而帶動毛利率結構性優化。基於毛利率假設上
修、3Q26 營收獲利優於預期、洽談中專案金額季增超過 20%，我們將中鼎
2026-27 年 EPS 上調至 6.64 元、7.09 元，上修幅度 24.6%、21.7%。 
能源政策轉向具共識，中鼎為國內少數具核電建廠實績與認證廠商。賴總統
上任以來核能政策逐漸轉向，並提到如有共識可重啟核電。我們認為一旦
11/28 非核家園公投通過，民意支持下，能源政策必將轉向擁核。後續包括
核三重啟、核四評估改建、引入 SMR(（Small Modular Reactor ）等都將成
為選項，預期將衍生數千億元龐大工程需求。中鼎為國內少數具發電廠及核
電廠建廠實績公司，有望深度參與能源轉向核能的商機。 
投資建議 
毛利率結構性提升具持續性，我們上修中鼎未來兩年獲利預估。且隨著大型
政府標案陸續進入工程期，預期 2027 年營收將恢復年增，營運動能顯著轉
強。我們將中鼎投資評等由「持有」上調至「增加持股」，目標價由 43 元
上調至 65 元。評價基於 2.1 倍 2027 年底每股淨值，位於十年歷史本淨比區
間中上緣，相當於 9.2 倍 2027 年本益比，或 7.1% 2026 年凱基預估配發現
金股息殖利率。 
投資風險 
應收帳款逾期、GEH 倒帳、三元電池廠訴訟案。

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 2025年中鼎在建工程存量大幅提升 
中鼎成立於 1979 年，為台灣最大的統包工程公司，提供客戶
工程規劃、設計、設備採購、施工、試車、操作、保養一貫化
的完整服務(EPC)。工程類型聚焦於煉油廠、石化廠、發電廠、
焚化爐。除工程外，集團透過轉投資子公司崑鼎(6803 TT，
NT$267，未評等)及新鼎(5209 TT，NT$167.5，未評等)等，跨
足綠能環保業務及系統整合業務。目前中鼎最新股本為 96.18
億元。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

## Document 581: 凱基投顧_DIS Disney_賴偉中_20261119

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_DIS Disney_賴偉中_20261119.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 19, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 娛樂‧美國

Disney 
(DIS.US/DIS US)

營運穩健成長，重獲資金青睞  
重要訊息 
迪士尼公布 4Q26 財報，調整稀釋後 EPS 為 1.77 元，高於市場共識 6.9%。 
評論及分析 
4Q26 獲利年增 57%，三大事業同時成長。迪士尼 4Q26 營業利益 40.7 億
元、年增 56.5%，EPS 為 1.77 元、YoY+59.5%，略優於市場預期 6.9%。
娛樂、體驗、體育三大事業貢獻獲利分別成長 92%、20%、53%。其中娛
樂部門成長主要來自 SVOD 串流業務，貢獻獲利成長至 8.2 億元、
YoY+105%、QoQ+14%，而體育部門成長則來自 NFL 及大學美式足球賽的
收視表現亮眼，帶動訂閱及廣告收入增加。 
2027 年展望正向，串流仍扮演獲利成長主力。我們對迪士尼 2027 年展望
正向，首先是體驗部門，巴黎迪士尼於 1Q26 啟用冰雪奇緣世界帶來強勁人
次成長，預期可延續至 2027 年，同時 2027 年華特迪士尼世界(WDW)也規
畫啟用怪獸電力公司及熱帶美洲主題園區，整體樂園獲利可望維持成長。娛
樂部門成長仍來自 SVOD，除訂閱人數成長外，Disney+及 Hulu 整合為單一
App 的工程已接近完成，上線後預期可增加用戶黏性，預估娛樂部門貢獻營
業利益可成長至 80.2 億元、YoY+31.9%。最後是體育部門，除 NFL 及大學
美式足球賽轉播外，EPSN 也已取得 2027 年美國超級盃(Super Bowl)的轉播
權，將聯合 ABC 頻道與 Disney+平台一起轉播，預期可帶動 ESPN 訂閱用戶
成長，預估貢獻營業利益為 33.5 億元、YoY+8.4%。 
投資建議 
AI 敘事備受考驗的投資環境下，營運穩定成長的百年老店迪士尼重新獲得投
資人青睞，帶動股價自 8 月低點上漲超過 30%。目前股價 122.4 元約交易
於 15.4 倍 2027 年本益比，位於歷史區間 13~24 倍中下緣。考量迪士尼獲
利處於上行階段，評價仍具修復空間，我們維持迪士尼「增加持股」投資評
等，目標價由 136 元上修至 141 元，評價維持 2027 年 18 倍本益比。 
投資風險 
疫情再起、郵輪及樂園意外事故、電影票房不佳、有線電視業務衰退加速。

Disney 成立於 1923 年，為全球最大的娛樂及媒體集團之一，並
被納入道瓊工業指數成分股、標普 500 指數成分股及那斯達克指
數成分股。迪士尼主要業務分為體驗(樂園、郵輪)、娛樂(影視、
媒體、串流平台)、運動(體育節目)。截至 2025 財年底迪士尼於
全球共有 6 個主題遊樂園區(含自有、JV 及授權)、6 艘自有郵
輪，並有 Disney+、Hulu、ESPN+等三個串流平台。旗下大型影
視 工作室包括 Pixar 、 WaltDisney 、 Marvel 、 Lucasfilm 、 20 
century studios、abc、National geographic 等。
